\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# {-}*{-} coding: utf{-}8 {-}*{-}}

\CommentTok{\# Sample Python code for youtube.search.list}
\CommentTok{\# See instructions for running these code samples locally:}
\CommentTok{\# https://developers.google.com/explorer{-}help/guides/code\_samples\#python}

\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ os}

\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ google\_auth\_oauthlib.flow}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ googleapiclient.discovery}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ googleapiclient.errors}

\NormalTok{scopes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{"https://www.googleapis.com/auth/youtube.force{-}ssl"}\NormalTok{]}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ main():}
    \CommentTok{\# Disable OAuthlib\textquotesingle{}s HTTPS verification when running locally.}
    \CommentTok{\# *DO NOT* leave this option enabled in production.}
\NormalTok{    os.environ[}\StringTok{"OAUTHLIB\_INSECURE\_TRANSPORT"}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"1"}

\NormalTok{    api\_service\_name }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"youtube"}
\NormalTok{    api\_version }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"v3"}
\NormalTok{    client\_secrets\_file }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"YOUR\_CLIENT\_SECRET\_FILE.json"}

    \CommentTok{\# Get credentials and create an API client}
\NormalTok{    flow }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ google\_auth\_oauthlib.flow.InstalledAppFlow.from\_client\_secrets\_file(}
\NormalTok{        client\_secrets\_file, scopes)}
\NormalTok{    credentials }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ flow.run\_console()}
\NormalTok{    youtube }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ googleapiclient.discovery.build(}
\NormalTok{        api\_service\_name, api\_version, credentials}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{credentials)}

\NormalTok{    request }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ youtube.search().}\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(}
\NormalTok{        part}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"snippet"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        maxResults}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{25}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{        q}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"surfing"}
\NormalTok{    )}
\NormalTok{    response }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ request.execute()}

    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(response)}

\ControlFlowTok{if} \VariableTok{\_\_name\_\_} \OperatorTok{==} \StringTok{"\_\_main\_\_"}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{    main()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Another way, but this is a dirty crawler, not for production

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ re, urllib}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ random }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ randint}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ random\_str(str\_size):}
\NormalTok{    res }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{""}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{xrange}\NormalTok{(str\_size):}
\NormalTok{        x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ randint(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{25}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        c }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{chr}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{ord}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}a\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{x)}
\NormalTok{        res }\OperatorTok{+=}\NormalTok{ c}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ res}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ find\_watch(text,pos):}
\NormalTok{    start }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ text.find(}\StringTok{"watch?v="}\NormalTok{,pos)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (start}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{):}
        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{,}\VariableTok{None}
\NormalTok{    end }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ text.find(}\StringTok{" "}\NormalTok{,start)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (end}\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{):}
        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{,}\VariableTok{None}

    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (end}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{start }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \DecValTok{200}\NormalTok{): }\CommentTok{\#silly heuristics, probably not a must}
        \ControlFlowTok{return} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{,}\VariableTok{None}

    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ text[start:end}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{], start}


\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ find\_instance\_links():}
\NormalTok{    base\_url }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}https://www.youtube.com/results?search\_query=\textquotesingle{}}
\NormalTok{    url }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ base\_url}\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{random\_str(}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}
    \CommentTok{\#print url}

\NormalTok{    r }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ urllib.urlopen(url).read()}

\NormalTok{    links }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}

\NormalTok{    pos }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}
    \ControlFlowTok{while} \VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        link,pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{  find\_watch(r,pos)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ link }\OperatorTok{==} \VariableTok{None} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ pos }\OperatorTok{==} \VariableTok{None}\NormalTok{:}
            \ControlFlowTok{break}
\NormalTok{        pos }\OperatorTok{+=} \DecValTok{1}
        \CommentTok{\#print link}
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (}\StringTok{";"} \KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ link):}
            \ControlFlowTok{continue}
\NormalTok{        links[link] }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1}

\NormalTok{    items\_list }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ links.items()}

\NormalTok{    list\_size }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(items\_list)}
\NormalTok{    selected }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ randint(list\_size}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{,list\_size}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ items\_list[selected][}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]}


\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{xrange}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{    sleep(randint(}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{)) }\CommentTok{\# pause randomly between 7 and 20 seconds}
\NormalTok{    link }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ find\_instance\_links()}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{ link}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{consumer-expenditure}{%
\section{Consumer Expenditure}\label{consumer-expenditure}}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  A consumer unit: ``a single person, or group of person who live together and who share responsibilities for most
  major expenditures.''
\item
  The ``Reference Person'' is \textbf{the first person named} when the respondent is asked, ``Who is responsible for owning or
  renting this home?''
\item
  2 components to the Consumer Expenditure:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Interview survey

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      for ``big ticket'' and recurring expenditures, and global estimates on others
    \item
      every three months for four quarter
    \item
      rotating-panel survey
    \item
      6,000 consumer units each quarter
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Diary survey

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      small-ticket, frequently purchased items
    \item
      independent from the interview survey
    \item
      about 14,000 diaries within families each year
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Table format

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    annual

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Jan to Dec: since 1984
    \item
      July to June: since 2013
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    two-year (Jan year 1 - Dec year 2)

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      since 1986
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Experimental research

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    all consumer units (available on request)
  \item
    E.g., income tables, generational groups (e.g., millennial, gen X)
  \end{itemize}
\item
\end{itemize}

\chapter{Modeling Application}\label{modeling-application}}

\section{Transformation}\label{transformation}}

\hypertarget{log-transformation}{%
\subsection{Log-transformation}\label{log-transformation}}

\citep{Manchanda_2004, Wies_2019} used 1 in place of 0 for log-transformation. And also use 0.5 and 0.0001 for sensitivity analysis.

To control for firm size effects, \citep{Wies_2019} scale advertising investments by the firm's total assets in the given year

\subsection{Control Function}\label{control-function}}

In the context of consumer choice model \citep{Petrin_2010}

\hypertarget{analytical-models}{%
\chapter{Analytical Models}\label{analytical-models}}

This theoretical/analytical model part of this section comes mostly from professor Murali Mantrala's Marketing Model Seminar.

Marketing models consists of

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Analytical Model: pure mathematical-based research
\item
  Empirical Model: data analysis.
\end{enumerate}

``A model is a representation of the most important elements of a perceived real-world system''.

Marketing model improves decision-making

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Econometric models

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Description
  \item
    Prediction
  \item
    Simulation
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Optimization models

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    maximize profit using market response model, cost functions, or any constraints.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Quasi- and Field experimental analyses
\item
  Conjoint Choice Experiments.
\end{itemize}

``A decision calculus will be defined as a model-based set of procedures for processing data and judgments to assist a manager in his decision making''\citep{Little_1976}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  simple
\item
  robust
\item
  easy to control
\item
  adaptive
\item
  as complete as possible
\item
  easy to communicate with
\end{itemize}

\citep{moorthy1993}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Mathematical Theoretical Models
\item
  Logical Experimentation
\item
  An environment as a model, specified by assumptions

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Math assumptions for tractability
  \item
    Substantive assumptions for empirical testing
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Decision support modeling describe how things work, and theoretical modeling present how things should work.
\item
  Compensation package including salaries and commission is a tradeoff between reduced income risk and motivation to work hard.
\item
  Internal and External Validity are questions related to the boundaries conditions of your experiments.
\item
  ``Theories are tested by their predictions, not by the realism of their super model assumptions.'' (Friedman, 1953)
\end{itemize}

\citep{mcafee1996}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Competition is performed under uncertainty
\item
  Competition reveals hidden information

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Independent-private-values case: selling price = second highest valuation
  \item
    It's always better for sellers to reveal information since it reduces chances of cautious bidding that is resulted from the winner's curse
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Competition is better than bargaining

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Competition requires less computation and commitment abilities
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Competition creates effort incentives
\end{itemize}

\citep{leeflang2000}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Types of model:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Predictive model

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Sales model: using time series data
    \item
      Trial rate: using exponential growth.
    \item
      Product growth model: \citet{Bass_1969}
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Descriptive model

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Purchase incidence and purchase timing : use Poisson process
    \item
      Brand choice: Markov models or learning models.
    \item
      Pricing decisions in an oligopolistic market \citet{howard1968}
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Normative model

    \begin{itemize}
    \tightlist
    \item
      Profit maximization based on price, adverting and quality \citep{dorfman1976}, extended by \citep{roberts1964, lambin1970a}
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

Later, \citet{Little_1970} introduced decision calculus and then multinomial logit model \citep{guadagni1983}

Potential marketing decision automation:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Promotion or pricing programs
\item
  Media allocation
\item
  Distribution
\item
  Product assortment
\item
  Direct mail solicitation
\end{itemize}

\citep{moorthy1985}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Definitions:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Rationality = maximizing subjective expected utility
  \item
    Intelligence = recognizing other firms are rational.
  \item
    Rules of the game include

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      \# of firms
    \item
      feasible set of actions
    \item
      utilities for each combination of moves
    \item
      sequence of moves
    \item
      the structure of info (who knows what and when?)
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Incomplete info stems from

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      unknown motivations
    \item
      unknown ability (capabilities)
    \item
      different knowledge of the world.
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Pure strategy = plan of action
  \item
    A mixed strategy = probability dist of pure strategies.
  \item
    Strategic form representation = sets of possible strategies for every firm and its payoffs.
  \item
    Equilibrium = a list of strategies in which ``no firm would like unilaterally to change its strategy.''
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Equilibrium is not outcome of a dynamic process.
\item
  Equilibrium Application

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Oligopolistic Competition

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Cournot (1838): quantities supplied: Cournot equilibrium. Changing quantities is more costly than changing prices
    \item
      Bertrand (1883): Bertrand equilibrium: pricing.
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Perfect competition
  \item
    Product Competition: Hotelling (1929): Principle of Minimum Differentiation is invalid.
  \item
    Entry:

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      first mover advantage
    \item
      deterrent strategy
    \item
      optimal for entrants or incumbents
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Channels
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Perfectness of equilibria

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Subgame perfectness
  \item
    Sequential rationality
  \item
    Trembling-hand perfectness
  \item
    Application

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Product and price competition in Oligopolies
    \item
      Strategic Entry Deterrence
    \end{itemize}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Dynamic games

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Long-term competition in oligopolies
  \item
    Implicit Collusion in practice : price match from leader firms
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Incomplete Information

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Durable goods pricing by a monopolist
  \item
    predatory pricing and limit pricing
  \item
    reputation, product quality, and prices
  \item
    Competitive bidding and auctions
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\citep{kim2006}: A location model with preference variety

\citep{hotelling1929}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Stability in competition
\item
  Duopoly is inherently unstable
\item
  Bertrand disagrees with Cournot, and Edgeworth elaborates on it.

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    because Cournot's assumption of absolutely identical products between firms.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

seller try to \(p_2 < p_1 c(l-a-b)\)

the point of indifference

\[
p_1 + cx = p_2 + cy
\]

\begin{itemize}
\item
  c = cost per unit of time in each unit of line length
\item
  p = price
\item
  q = quantity
\item
  x, y = length from A and B respectively
\end{itemize}

\[
a + x + y + b = l
\]

is the length of the street

Hence, we have

\[
x = 0.5(l - a - b + \frac{p_2- p_1}{c}) \\
y = 0.5(l - a - b + \frac{p_1- p_2}{c})
\]

Profits will be

\[
\pi_1 = p_1 q_1 = p_1 (a+ x) = 0.5 (l + a - b) p_1 - \frac{p_1^2}{2c} + \frac{p_1 p_2}{2c} \\
\pi_2 = p_2 q_2 = p_2 (b+ y) = 0.5 (l + a - b) p_2 - \frac{p_2^2}{2c} + \frac{p_1 p_2}{2c}
\]

To set the price to maximize profit, we have

\[
\frac{\partial \pi_1}{\partial p_1} = 0.5 (l + a - b) - \frac{p_1}{c} + \frac{p_2}{2c} = 0 \\
\frac{\partial \pi_2}{\partial p_2} = 0.5 (l - a + b) - \frac{p_2}{c} + \frac{p_1}{2c} = 0
\]

which equals

\[
p_1 = c(l + \frac{a-b}{3}) \\
p_2 = c(l - \frac{a-b}{3})
\]

and

\[
q_1 = a + x = 0.5 (l + \frac{a -b}{3}) \\
q_2 = b + y = 0.5 (l - \frac{a-b}{3})
\]

with the SOC satisfied

In case of deciding locations, socialism works better than capitalism

\citep{daspremont1979}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Principle of Minimum Differentiation is invalid
\end{itemize}

\[
\pi_1 (p_1, p_2) = 
\begin{cases}
ap_1 + 0.5(l-a-b) p_1 + \frac{1}{2c}p_1 p_2 - \frac{1}{2c}p_1^2 & \text{if } |p_1 - p_2| \le c(l-a-b) \\
lp_1 & \text{if } p_1 < p_2 - c(l-a-b) \\
0 & \text{if } p_1 > p_2 + c(l-a-b)
\end{cases}
\]

and

\[
\pi_2 (p_1, p_2) = 
\begin{cases}
bp_2 + 0.5(l-a-b) p_2 + \frac{1}{2c}p_1 p_2  - \frac{1}{2c}p_2^2& \text{if } |p_1 - p_2| \le c(l-a-b) \\
lp_2 & \text{if } p_2 < p_1 - c(l-a-b) \\
0 & \text{if } p_2 > p_1 + c(l-a-b)
\end{cases}
\]

\hypertarget{positioning-models}{%
\section{Positioning Models}\label{positioning-models}}

\citet{tabuchi1995}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Relax Hotelling's model's assumption of uniform distribution of consumers to non-uniform distribution.
\item
  Assumptions:

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Equal cost
  \item
    Consumers distributed over {[}0,1{]}
  \item
    \(F(x)\) = cumulative distribution of consumers where \(F(1) = 1\) = total population
  \item
    2 distributions:

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Traditional uniform density: \(f(x) =1\)
    \item
      New: triangular density: \(f(x) = 2 - 2|2x-1|\) which represents consumer concentration
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    Transportation cost = quadratic function of distance.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

Hence, marginal consumer is

\[
\bar{x} = (p_2 - p_1 + x^2_2-x_1^2)/2(x_2-x_1)
\]

then when \(x_1 < x_2\) the profit function is

\[
\Pi_1 = p_1 F(\bar{x})
\]

and

\[
\Pi_2 = p_2[1-F(\bar{x})]
\]

and vice versa for \(x_1 >x_2\), and Bertrand game when \(x_1 = x_2\)

\begin{itemize}
\item
  If firms pick \textbf{simultaneously} their locations, and then \textbf{simultaneously} their prices, and consumer density function is log-concave, then there is a unique Nash price equilibrium

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Under \textbf{uniform} distribution, firms choose to locate as far apart as possible (could be true when observing shopping centers are far away from cities), but then consumers have to buy products that are far away from their ideal.
  \item
    Under \textbf{triangular} density, no symmetric location can be found, but two asymmetric Nash location equilibrium can still be possible (decrease in equilibrium profits of both firms)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  If firms pick \textbf{sequentially} their locations, and pick their prices \textbf{simultaneously,}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Under both uniform and triangular, first entrant will locate at the market center
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\citet{sajeesh2010}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Model satiation (variety-seeking) as a relative reduction in the willingness to pay of the previously purchased brand. also known as negative state dependence
\item
  Previous studies argue that in the presence of variety seeking consumers, firms should enjoy higher prices and profits, but this paper argues that average prices and profits are lower.

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Firms should charge lower prices in the second period to prevent consumers from switching.
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

Assumptions:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Period 0, choose location simultaneously
\item
  Period 1, choose prices simultaneously
\item
  Period 2, firms choose prices simultaneously
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{market-response-model}{%
\section{Market Response Model}\label{market-response-model}}

Marketing Inputs:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Selling effort
\item
  advertising spending
\item
  promotional spending
\end{itemize}

\[
\downarrow
\]

Marketing Outputs:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  sales
\item
  share
\item
  profit
\item
  awareness
\end{itemize}

\href{https://slideplayer.com/slide/5259893/}{\includegraphics{images/Input-Output+Model+Marketing+Actions+Inputs+Observed+Market+Outputs.jpg}}

Give phenomena for a good model:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  P1: Dynamic sales response involves a sales \textbf{growth rate} and a sales \textbf{decay rate} that are different
\item
  P2: Steady-state response can be \textbf{concave or S-shaped}. Positive sales at 0 adverting.
\item
  P3: \textbf{Competitive effects}
\item
  P4: Advertising effectiveness dynamics due to changes in media, copy, and other factors.
\item
  P5: Sales still increase or fall off even as advertising is held constant.
\end{itemize}

Saunder (1987) phenomena

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  P1: Output = 0 when Input = 0
\item
  P2: The relationship between input and output is linear
\item
  P3: Returns decrease as the scale of input increases (i.e., additional unit of input gives less output)
\item
  P4: Output cannot exceed some level (i.e., saturation)
\item
  P5: Returns increase as scale of input increases (i.e., additional unit of input gives more output)
\item
  P6: Returns first increase and then decrease as input increases (i.e., S-shaped return)
\item
  P7: Input must exceed some level before it produces any output (i.e., threshold)
\item
  P8: Beyond some level of input, output declines (i.e., supersaturation point)
\end{itemize}

\href{https://slideplayer.com/slide/4552247/}{\includegraphics{images/Response+Function+Sales+Response+Effort+Level+Max+Response+Function.jpg}}

Aggregate Response Models

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Linear model: \(Y = a + bX\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Through origin
  \item
    can only handle constant returns to scale (i.e., can't handle concave, convex, and S-shape)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  The Power Series/Polynomial model: \(Y = a + bX + c X^2 + dX^3 + ...\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    can't handle saturation and threshold
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Fraction root model/ Power model: \(Y = a+bX^c\) where c is prespecified

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    c = 1/2, called \textbf{square root model}
  \item
    c = -1, called \textbf{reciprocal model}
  \item
    c can be interpreted as elasticity if a = 0.
  \item
    c = 1, linear
  \item
    c \textless1, decreasing return
  \item
    c\textgreater1, increasing returns
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Semilog model: \(Y = a + b \ln X\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Good when constant percentage increase in marketing effort (X) result in constant absolute increase in sales (Y)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Exponential model: \(Y = ae^{bX}\) where X \textgreater0

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    b \textgreater{} 0, increasing returns and convex
  \item
    b \textless{} 0, decreasing returns and saturation
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Modified exponential model: \(Y = a(1-e^{-bX}) +c\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    Decreasing returns and saturation
  \item
    upper bound = a + c
  \item
    lower bound = c
  \item
    typically used in selling effort
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Logistic model: \(Y = \frac{a}{a+ e^{-(b+cX)}}+d\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    increasing return followed by decreasing return to scale, S-shape
  \item
    saturation = a + d
  \item
    good with saturation and s-shape
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Gompertz model
\item
  ADBUDG model \citep{Little_1970} : \(Y = b + (a-b)\frac{X^c}{d + X^c}\)

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    c \textgreater{} 1, S-shaped
  \item
    0 \textless{} c \textless{} 1

    \begin{itemize}
    \item
      Concave
    \item
      saturation effect
    \item
      upper bound at a
    \item
      lower bound at b
    \end{itemize}
  \item
    typically used in advertising and selling effort.
  \item
    can handle, through origin, concave, saturation, S-shape
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Additive model for handling multiple Instruments: \(Y = af(X_1) + bg(X_2)\)
\item
  Multiplicative model for handling multiple instruments: \(Y = aX_1^b X_2^c\) where c and c are elasticities. More generally, \(Y = af(X_1)\times bg(X_2)\)
\item
  Multiplicative and additive model: \(Y = af(X_1) + bg(X_2) + cf(X_1) g(X_2)\)
\item
  Dynamic response model: \(Y_t = a_0 + a_1 X_t + \lambda Y_{t-1}\) where \(a_1\) = current effect, \(\lambda\) = carry-over effect
\end{itemize}

Dynamic Effects

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Carry-over effect: current marketing expenditure influences future sales

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Advertising adstock/ advertising carry-over is the same thing: lagged effect of advertising on sales
  \end{itemize}
\item
  Delayed-response effect: delays between when marketing investments and their impact
\item
  Customer holdout effects
\item
  Hysteresis effect
\item
  New trier and wear-out effect
\item
  Stocking effect
\end{itemize}

Simple Decay-effect model:

\[
A_t = T_t + \lambda T_{t-1}, t = 1,...,
\]

where

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(A_t\) = Adstock at time t
\item
  \(T_t\) = value of advertising spending at time t
\item
  \(\lambda\) = decay/ lag weight parameter
\end{itemize}

Response Models can be characterized by:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  The number of marketing variables
\item
  whether they include competition or not
\item
  the nature of the relationship between the input variables

  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Linear vs.~S-shape
  \end{enumerate}
\item
  whether the situation is static vs.~dynamic
\item
  whether the models reflect individual or aggregate response
\item
  the level of demand analyzed

  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumii{\arabic{enumii}.}
  \tightlist
  \item
    sales vs.~market share
  \end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}

Market Share Model and Competitive Effects: \(Y = M \times V\) where

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Y = Brand sales models
\item
  V = product class sales models
\item
  M = market-share models
\end{itemize}

Market share (attraction) models

\[
M_i = \frac{A_i}{A_1 + ..+ A_n}
\]

where \(A_i\) attractiveness of brand i

Individual Response Model:

Multinomial logit model representing the probability of individual \texttt{i} choosing brand \texttt{l} is

\[
P_{il} = \frac{e^{A_{il}}}{\sum_j e^{A_{ij}}}
\]

where

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(A_{ij}\) = attractiveness of product j for individual i \(A_{ij} = \sum_k w_k b_{ijk}\)
\item
  \(b_{ijk}\) = individual i's evaluation of product j on product attribute k, where the summation is over all the products that individual \texttt{i} is considering to purchase
\item
  \(w_k\) = importance weight associated with attribute k in forming product preferences.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{marketing-resource-allocation-models}{%
\section{Marketing Resource Allocation Models}\label{marketing-resource-allocation-models}}

This section is based on \citep{Mantrala_1992}

\hypertarget{case-study-1}{%
\subsection{Case study 1}\label{case-study-1}}

Concave sales response function

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Optimal vs.~proportional at different investment levels
\item
  Profit maximization perspective of aggregate function
\end{itemize}

\[
s_i = k_i (1- e^{-b_i x_i})
\]

where

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(s_i\) = current-period sales response (dollars / period)
\item
  \(x_i\) = amount of resource allocated to submarket i
\item
  \(b_i\) = rate at which sales approach saturation
\item
  \(k_i\) = sales potential
\end{itemize}

Allocation functions

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Fixed proportion

  \begin{itemize}
  \item
    \(R_i\) = Investment level (dollars/period)
  \item
    \(w_i\) = fixed proportion or weights
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\[
\hat{x}_i = w_i R; \\
\sum_{t=1}^2 w_t = 1; 0 < w_t < 1
\]

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Informed allocator

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    optimal allocations via marginal analysis (maximize profits)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\[
max C = m \sum_{i = 1}^2 k_i (1- e^{-b_i x_i}) \\
x_1 + x_2 \le R; x_i  \ge 0 \text{ for } i = 1,2 \\
x_1 = \frac{1}{(b_1 + b_2)(b_2 R + \ln(\frac{k_1b_1}{k_2b_2})} \\
x_2 = \frac{1}{(b_1 + b_2)(b_2 R + \ln(\frac{k_2b_2}{k_1b_1})}
\]

\hypertarget{case-study-2}{%
\subsection{Case study 2}\label{case-study-2}}

S-shaped sales response function:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Optimal vs.~proportional at different investment levels
\item
  Profit maximization perspective of aggregate function
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{case-study-3}{%
\subsection{Case study 3}\label{case-study-3}}

Quadratic-form stochastic response function

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Optimal allocation only with risk averse and risk neutral investors.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{ordered-shapley}{%
\subsection{Ordered Shapley}\label{ordered-shapley}}

Based on \citep{Zhao_2018} (to access \href{https://arxiv.org/ftp/arxiv/papers/1804/1804.05327.pdf}{paper}) Cooperative game theory: look at the marginal contribution of each player in the game, where \textbf{Shapley value} (i..e, the credit assigned to each individual) is the expected value value of the marginal contribution over all possible permutations (e.g., all possible sequences) of the players.

Shapely value considered:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  marginal contribution of each player (i.e., channel)
\item
  sequence of joining the coalition (i.e., customer journey).
\end{itemize}

Typically, we can't apply the Shapley Value method due to computational burden (you need all possible permutations). And a drawback is that all the credit must be divided among your channels, if you have missing channels, then it will distort the estimates of other channels' estimates.

It's hard to use Shapley value model for model comparison since we have no ``ground truth''

Marketing application:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \href{\%5Bhttps://salesforceben.com/the-drip/complete-overview-of-pardot-einstein-features\%5D(https://salesforceben.com/the-drip/complete-overview-of-pardot-einstein-features/)}{Pardot Einstein features} and \href{https://www.salesforceben.com/the-drip/pardot-einstein-attribution-a-deeper-dive/}{more}
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{markov-model}{%
\subsection{Markov Model}\label{markov-model}}

Markov chains maps the movement and gives a probability distribution, for moving from one state to another state. A Markov Chain has three properties:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{State space} -- set of all the states in which process could potentially exist
\item
  \textbf{Transition operator} --the probability of moving from one state to other state
\item
  \textbf{Current state probability distribution} -- probability distribution of being in any one of the states at the start of the process
\end{itemize}

In mathematically sense

\[
w_{ij}= P(X_t = s_j|X_{t-1}=s_i),0 \le w_{ij} \le 1, \sum_{j=1}^N w_{ij} =1 \forall i
\]

where

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Transition Probability (\(w_{ij}\)) = The Probability of the Previous State ( \(X_{t-1}\)) Given the Current State (\(X_t\))
\item
  The Transition Probability (\(w_{ij}\)) is No Less Than 0 and No Greater Than 1
\item
  The Sum of the Transition Probabilities Equals 1 (i.e., Everyone Must Go Somewhere)
\end{itemize}

To examine a particular node in the Markov graph, we use \textbf{removal effect} (\(s_i\)) to see its contribution to a conversion. In another word, the Removal Effect is the probability of converting when a step is completely removed; all sequences that had to go through that step are now sent directly to the null node

Each node is called \textbf{transition states}\\
The probability of moving from one channel to another channel is called \textbf{transition probability}.

first-order or ``memory-free'' Markov graph is called ``memory-free'' because the probability of reaching one state depends only on the previous state visited.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Order 0: Do not care about where the you came from or what step the you are on, only the probability of going to any state.
\item
  Order 1: Looks back zero steps. You are currently at a state. The probability of going anywhere is based on being at that step.
\item
  Order 2: Looks back one step. You came from state A and are currently at state B. The probability of going anywhere is based on where you were and where you are.
\item
  Order 3: Looks back two steps. You came from state A after state B and are currently at state C. The probability of going anywhere is based on where you were and where you are.
\item
  Order 4: Looks back three steps. You came from state A after B after C and are currently at state D. The probability of going anywhere is based on where you were and where you are.
\end{itemize}

\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\#Read the data into R}
\NormalTok{channel }\OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{read.csv}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"images/Channel\_attribution.csv"}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{header =}\NormalTok{ T) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%} \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(Output))}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(channel, }\AttributeTok{n =} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
##   R05A.01 R05A.02 R05A.03 R05A.04 R05A.05 R05A.06 R05A.07 R05A.08 R05A.09
## 1      16       4       3       5      10       8       6       8      13
## 2       2       1       9      10       1       4       3      21      NA
##   R05A.10 R05A.11 R05A.12 R05A.13 R05A.14 R05A.15 R05A.16 R05A.17 R05A.18
## 1      20      21      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA
## 2      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA      NA
##   R05A.19 R05A.20
## 1      NA      NA
## 2      NA      NA
\end{verbatim}

The number represents:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  1-19 are various channels
\item
  20 -- customer has decided which device to buy;
\item
  21 -- customer has made the final purchase, and;
\item
  22 -- customer hasn't decided yet.
\end{itemize}

Pre-processing

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ (row }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\FunctionTok{nrow}\NormalTok{(channel))\{}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{21} \SpecialCharTok{\%in\%}\NormalTok{ channel[row,])\{}
\NormalTok{        channel}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{convert }\OtherTok{=} \DecValTok{1}
\NormalTok{    \}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\NormalTok{column }\OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(channel)}
\NormalTok{channel}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path }\OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{do.call}\NormalTok{(paste, }\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(channel, }\AttributeTok{sep =} \StringTok{" \textgreater{} "}\NormalTok{))}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(channel}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## [1] "16 > 4 > 3 > 5 > 10 > 8 > 6 > 8 > 13 > 20 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"     
## [2] "2 > 1 > 9 > 10 > 1 > 4 > 3 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"     
## [3] "9 > 13 > 20 > 16 > 15 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"
## [4] "8 > 15 > 20 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"
## [5] "16 > 9 > 13 > 20 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"
## [6] "1 > 11 > 8 > 4 > 9 > 21 > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > NA > 1"
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{(row }\ControlFlowTok{in} \DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\FunctionTok{nrow}\NormalTok{(channel))\{}
\NormalTok{  channel}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path[row] }\OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{strsplit}\NormalTok{(channel}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path[row], }\StringTok{" \textgreater{} 21"}\NormalTok{)[[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]][}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{\}}
\NormalTok{channel\_fin }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ channel[,}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{22}\NormalTok{,}\DecValTok{21}\NormalTok{)]}
\NormalTok{channel\_fin }\OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{ddply}\NormalTok{(channel\_fin,}\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{path,summarise, }\AttributeTok{conversion=} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(convert))}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(channel\_fin)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

## 5          1 > 1 > 3 > 17 > 17          1
## 6 1 > 1 > 6 > 1 > 12 > 20 > 12          1
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}

\NormalTok{Data }\OtherTok{=}\NormalTok{ channel\_fin}

\NormalTok{H}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  First Touch Conversion: credit is given to the first touch point.
\item
  Last Touch Conversion: credit is given to the last touch point.
\item
  Linear Touch Conversion: All channels/touch points are given equal credit in the conversion.
\end{itemize}

Markov model

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{M }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{markov\_model}\NormalTok{(Data, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conversion\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{var\_value=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conversion\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{order =} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}

combine the two models

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Merges the two data frames on the "channel\_name" column.}
\NormalTok{R }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{merge}\NormalTok{(H, M, }\AttributeTok{by=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Select only relevant columns}
\NormalTok{R1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ R[, (}\FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(R) }\SpecialCharTok{\%in\%} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}first\_touch\_conversions\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}last\_touch\_conversions\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}linear\_touch\_conversions\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}total\_conversion\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{))]}

\CommentTok{\# Transforms the dataset into a data frame that ggplot2 can use to plot the outcomes}
\NormalTok{R1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{melt}\NormalTok{(R1, }\AttributeTok{id=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Plot the total conversions}
\FunctionTok{ggplot}\NormalTok{(R1, }\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(channel\_name, value, }\AttributeTok{fill =}\NormalTok{ variable)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_bar}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{stat=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}identity\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{position=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dodge\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{ggtitle}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}TOTAL CONVERSIONS\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{axis.title.x =} \FunctionTok{element\_text}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{vjust =} \SpecialCharTok{{-}}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{axis.title.y =} \FunctionTok{element\_text}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{vjust =} \SpecialCharTok{+}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{title =} \FunctionTok{element\_text}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{size =} \DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{plot.title=}\FunctionTok{element\_text}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{size =} \DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{ylab}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{""}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

and then check the final results.

\hypertarget{example-2}{%
\subsubsection{Example 2}\label{example-2}}

Example code by \href{https://www.analyzecore.com/2016/08/03/attribution-model-r-part-1/}{Sergey Bryl'}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(dplyr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(reshape2)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggplot2)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggthemes)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggrepel)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(RColorBrewer)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ChannelAttribution)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(markovchain)}
 
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\#\#\# simple example \#\#\#\#\#}
\CommentTok{\# creating a data sample}
\NormalTok{df1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c1 \textgreater{} c2 \textgreater{} c3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c2 \textgreater{} c3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{), }\AttributeTok{conv =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{), }\AttributeTok{conv\_null =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{))}
 
\CommentTok{\# calculating the model}
\NormalTok{mod1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{markov\_model}\NormalTok{(df1,}
                    \AttributeTok{var\_path =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                    \AttributeTok{var\_conv =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                    \AttributeTok{var\_null =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv\_null\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                    \AttributeTok{out\_more =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)}
 
\CommentTok{\# extracting the results of attribution}
\NormalTok{df\_res1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ mod1}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result}
 
\CommentTok{\# extracting a transition matrix}
\NormalTok{df\_trans1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ mod1}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{transition\_matrix}
\NormalTok{df\_trans1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{dcast}\NormalTok{(df\_trans1, channel\_from }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ channel\_to, }\AttributeTok{value.var =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}transition\_probability\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
 
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\# plotting the Markov graph }\AlertTok{\#\#\#}
\NormalTok{df\_trans }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ mod1}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{transition\_matrix}
 
\CommentTok{\# adding dummies in order to plot the graph}
\NormalTok{df\_dummy }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{channel\_from =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(conversion)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(null)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{),}
                       \AttributeTok{channel\_to =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(conversion)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(null)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{),}
                       \AttributeTok{transition\_probability =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{df\_trans }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{rbind}\NormalTok{(df\_trans, df\_dummy)}
 
\CommentTok{\# ordering channels}
\NormalTok{df\_trans}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel\_from }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{factor}\NormalTok{(df\_trans}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel\_from,}\AttributeTok{levels =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(conversion)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(null)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{df\_trans}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel\_to }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{factor}\NormalTok{(df\_trans}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel\_to,}\AttributeTok{levels =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(conversion)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(null)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}c3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{df\_trans }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{dcast}\NormalTok{(df\_trans, channel\_from }\SpecialCharTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{ channel\_to, }\AttributeTok{value.var =}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}transition\_probability\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
 
\CommentTok{\# creating the markovchain object}
\NormalTok{trans\_matrix }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{matrix}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{data =} \FunctionTok{as.matrix}\NormalTok{(df\_trans[, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]),}\AttributeTok{nrow =} \FunctionTok{nrow}\NormalTok{(df\_trans[, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]), }\AttributeTok{ncol =} \FunctionTok{ncol}\NormalTok{(df\_trans[, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]),}\AttributeTok{dimnames =} \FunctionTok{list}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{as.character}\NormalTok{(df\_trans[,}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])),}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(df\_trans[, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]))))}
\NormalTok{trans\_matrix[}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(trans\_matrix)] }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \DecValTok{0}
\CommentTok{\# trans\_matrix1 \textless{}{-} new("markovchain", transitionMatrix = trans\_matrix)}
\CommentTok{\# }
\CommentTok{\# \# plotting the graph}
\CommentTok{\# plot(trans\_matrix1, edge.arrow.size = 0.35)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# simulating the "real" data}
\FunctionTok{set.seed}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{354}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{df2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{client\_id =} \FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{5000}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{),}
                  \AttributeTok{date =} \FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{5000}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{),}
                  \AttributeTok{channel =} \FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{5000}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{,}
                                   \AttributeTok{prob =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.15}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.07}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.11}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.07}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.13}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.06}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.16}\NormalTok{)))}
\NormalTok{df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{date }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{as.Date}\NormalTok{(df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{date, }\AttributeTok{origin =} \StringTok{"2015{-}01{-}01"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, df2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{channel)}
 
\CommentTok{\# aggregating channels to the paths for each customer}
\NormalTok{df2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df2 }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{arrange}\NormalTok{(client\_id, date) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(client\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path =} \FunctionTok{paste}\NormalTok{(channel, }\AttributeTok{collapse =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{),}
                  \CommentTok{\# assume that all paths were finished with conversion}
                  \AttributeTok{conv =} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{,}
                  \AttributeTok{conv\_null =} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
 
\CommentTok{\# calculating the models (Markov and heuristics)}
\NormalTok{mod2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{markov\_model}\NormalTok{(df2,}
                     \AttributeTok{var\_path =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                     \AttributeTok{var\_conv =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                     \AttributeTok{var\_null =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv\_null\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                     \AttributeTok{out\_more =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## 
## Number of simulations: 100000 - Convergence reached: 1.40% < 5.00%
## 
## Percentage of simulated paths that successfully end before maximum number of steps (13) is reached: 95.98%
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# heuristic\_models() function doesn\textquotesingle{}t work for me, therefore I used the manual calculations}
\CommentTok{\# instead of:}
\CommentTok{\#h\_mod2 \textless{}{-} heuristic\_models(df2, var\_path = \textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}, var\_conv = \textquotesingle{}conv\textquotesingle{})}
 
\NormalTok{df\_hm }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df2 }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{channel\_name\_ft =} \FunctionTok{sub}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textgreater{}.*\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, path),}
               \AttributeTok{channel\_name\_ft =} \FunctionTok{sub}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, channel\_name\_ft),}
               \AttributeTok{channel\_name\_lt =} \FunctionTok{sub}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}.*\textgreater{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, path),}
               \AttributeTok{channel\_name\_lt =} \FunctionTok{sub}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, channel\_name\_lt))}
\CommentTok{\# first{-}touch conversions}
\NormalTok{df\_ft }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_hm }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(channel\_name\_ft) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{first\_touch\_conversions =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(conv)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
\CommentTok{\# last{-}touch conversions}
\NormalTok{df\_lt }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_hm }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(channel\_name\_lt) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{last\_touch\_conversions =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(conv)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
 
\NormalTok{h\_mod2 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{merge}\NormalTok{(df\_ft, df\_lt, }\AttributeTok{by.x =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\_ft\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{by.y =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\_lt\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
 
\CommentTok{\# merging all models}
\NormalTok{all\_models }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{merge}\NormalTok{(h\_mod2, mod2}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result, }\AttributeTok{by.x =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\_ft\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{by.y =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(all\_models)[}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)] }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}attrib\_model\_conversions\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\NormalTok{df\_raw }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{customer\_id =} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}id\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{20000}\NormalTok{), }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)), }\AttributeTok{date =} \FunctionTok{as.Date}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{rbeta}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{80000}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{*} \DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{origin =} \StringTok{"2016{-}01{-}01"}\NormalTok{), }\AttributeTok{channel =} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\SpecialCharTok{:}\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{80000}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{prob =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{0.2}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.12}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.03}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.07}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.15}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.25}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.08}\NormalTok{))) ) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{conversion =} \FunctionTok{sample}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{), }\FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{(), }\AttributeTok{prob =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FloatTok{0.975}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.025}\NormalTok{), }\AttributeTok{replace =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{dmap\_at}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{), as.character) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{arrange}\NormalTok{(customer\_id, date)}
 
\NormalTok{df\_raw }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_raw }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{channel =} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(channel }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\ConstantTok{NA}\NormalTok{, channel))}

\hypertarget{customers-will-be-at-different-stage-of-purchase-journey-after-each-conversion.}{%
\paragraph{1. Customers will be at different stage of purchase journey after each conversion.}\label{customers-will-be-at-different-stage-of-purchase-journey-after-each-conversion.}}

First-time buyer's journey will look different from n-times buyer's (e.g., he will not start at website )

You can create your own code to split data into customers in different stages.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\#\#\# splitting paths \#\#\#\#\#}
\NormalTok{df\_paths }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_raw }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path\_no =} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{lag}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{cumsum}\NormalTok{(conversion))), }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{lag}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{cumsum}\NormalTok{(conversion))) }\SpecialCharTok{+} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%} \CommentTok{\# add the path\textquotesingle{}s serial number by using the lagged cumulative sum of conversion binary marks}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
\FunctionTok{head}\NormalTok{(df\_paths)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## # A tibble: 6 x 5
##   customer_id date       channel   conversion path_no
##   <chr>       <date>     <chr>          <dbl>   <dbl>
## 1 id1         2016-01-02 channel_7          0       1
## 2 id1         2016-01-09 channel_4          0       1
## 3 id1         2016-01-18 channel_5          1       1
## 4 id1         2016-01-20 channel_4          1       2
## 5 id100       2016-01-01 channel_0          0       1
## 6 id100       2016-01-01 channel_0          0       1
\end{verbatim}

attribution path for first-time buyers:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{df\_paths\_1 }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_paths }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(path\_no }\SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{path\_no)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{handle-missing-data}{%
\paragraph{2. Handle missing data}\label{handle-missing-data}}

We might have missing data on the channel or do not want to attribute a path (e.g., Direct Channel). We can either

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Remove NA/Channel
\item
  Use the previous channel in its place.
\end{itemize}

In the first-order Markov chains, the results are unchanged because duplicated channels don't affect the calculation.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\#\#\# replace some channels \#\#\#\#\#}
\NormalTok{df\_path\_1\_clean }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_paths\_1 }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \CommentTok{\# removing NAs}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{!}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(channel)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
         
        \CommentTok{\# adding order of channels in the path}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{ord =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{()),}
               \AttributeTok{is\_non\_direct =} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(channel }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_6\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{),}
               \AttributeTok{is\_non\_direct\_cum =} \FunctionTok{cumsum}\NormalTok{(is\_non\_direct)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
         
        \CommentTok{\# removing Direct (channel\_6) when it is the first in the path}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(is\_non\_direct\_cum }\SpecialCharTok{!=} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
         
        \CommentTok{\# replacing Direct (channel\_6) with the previous touch point}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{channel =} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(channel }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_6\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, channel[}\FunctionTok{which}\NormalTok{(channel }\SpecialCharTok{!=} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_6\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)][is\_non\_direct\_cum], channel)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
         
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ord, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{is\_non\_direct, }\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{is\_non\_direct\_cum)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\hypertarget{one-vs.-multi-channel-paths}{%
\paragraph{3. one vs.~multi-channel paths}\label{one-vs.-multi-channel-paths}}

We need to calculate the weighted importance for each channel because the sum of the Removal Effects doesn't equal to 1. In case we have a path with a unique channel, the Removal Effect and importance of this channel for that exact path is 1. However, weighting with other multi-channel paths will decrease the importance of one-channel occurrences. That means that, in case we have a channel that occurs in one-channel paths, usually it will be underestimated if attributed with multi-channel paths.

There is also a pretty straight logic behind splitting -- for one-channel paths, we definitely know the channel that brought a conversion and we don't need to distribute that value into other channels.

To account for one-channel path:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Split data for paths with one or more unique channels
\item
  Calculate total conversions for one-channel paths and compute the Markov model for multi-channel paths
\item
  Summarize results for each channel.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\#\#\# one{-} and multi{-}channel paths \#\#\#\#\#}
\NormalTok{df\_path\_1\_clean }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{uniq\_channel\_tag =} \FunctionTok{ifelse}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{length}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{unique}\NormalTok{(channel)) }\SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{, }\ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
 
\NormalTok{df\_path\_1\_clean\_uniq }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(uniq\_channel\_tag }\SpecialCharTok{==} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{uniq\_channel\_tag)}
 
\NormalTok{df\_path\_1\_clean\_multi }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(uniq\_channel\_tag }\SpecialCharTok{==} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{{-}}\NormalTok{uniq\_channel\_tag)}
 
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\# experiment }\AlertTok{\#\#\#}
\CommentTok{\# attribution model for all paths}
\NormalTok{df\_all\_paths }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path =} \FunctionTok{paste}\NormalTok{(channel, }\AttributeTok{collapse =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{),}
                  \AttributeTok{conversion =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(conversion)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(conversion }\SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
 
\NormalTok{mod\_attrib }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{markov\_model}\NormalTok{(df\_all\_paths,}
                           \AttributeTok{var\_path =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                           \AttributeTok{var\_conv =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conversion\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                           \AttributeTok{out\_more =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
## 
## Number of simulations: 100000 - Convergence reached: 1.28% < 5.00%
## 
## Percentage of simulated paths that successfully end before maximum number of steps (19) is reached: 99.92%
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{mod\_attrib}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{removal\_effects}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

## 1    channel_7       0.2812250
## 2    channel_4       0.4284428
## 3    channel_5       0.6056845
## 4    channel_0       0.5367294
## 5    channel_1       0.3820056
## 6    channel_3       0.2535028
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}

## 1    channel_7          192.8653
## 2    channel_4          293.8279
## 3    channel_5          415.3811
## 4    channel_0          368.0913
## 5    channel_1          261.9811
## 6    channel_3          173.8533
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{d\_all }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(mod\_attrib}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result)}
 
\CommentTok{\# attribution model for splitted multi and unique channel paths}
\NormalTok{df\_multi\_paths }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean\_multi }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(customer\_id) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path =} \FunctionTok{paste}\NormalTok{(channel, }\AttributeTok{collapse =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{),}
                  \AttributeTok{conversion =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(conversion)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(conversion }\SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
 
\NormalTok{mod\_attrib\_alt }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{markov\_model}\NormalTok{(df\_multi\_paths,}
                           \AttributeTok{var\_path =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}path\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                           \AttributeTok{var\_conv =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conversion\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
                           \AttributeTok{out\_more =} \ConstantTok{TRUE}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}

## Number of simulations: 100000 - Convergence reached: 1.21% < 5.00%
## 
## Percentage of simulated paths that successfully end before maximum number of steps (19) is reached: 99.59%
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{mod\_attrib\_alt}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{removal\_effects}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

## 3    channel_5       0.6526369
## 4    channel_0       0.5814164
## 5    channel_1       0.4343546
## 6    channel_3       0.2898041
\end{verbatim}

\NormalTok{mod\_attrib\_alt}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result}

\begin{verbatim}

## 1    channel_7          150.9460
## 2    channel_4          223.9350

## 4    channel_0          268.7406
## 5    channel_1          200.7661
## 6    channel_3          133.9524
\end{verbatim}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# adding unique paths}
\NormalTok{df\_uniq\_paths }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_path\_1\_clean\_uniq }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(conversion }\SpecialCharTok{==} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(channel) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{conversions =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(conversion)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
 
\NormalTok{d\_multi }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{data.frame}\NormalTok{(mod\_attrib\_alt}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result)}
 
\NormalTok{d\_split }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{full\_join}\NormalTok{(d\_multi, df\_uniq\_paths, }\AttributeTok{by =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_name\textquotesingle{}} \OtherTok{=} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{result =}\NormalTok{ total\_conversions }\SpecialCharTok{+}\NormalTok{ conversions)}
 
\FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(d\_all}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{total\_conversions)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(d\_split}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{result)}

\hypertarget{higher-order-markov-chains}{%
\paragraph{4. Higher Order Markov Chains}\label{higher-order-markov-chains}}

Since the transition matrix stays the same in the first order Markov, having duplicates will not affect the result. But starting from the second order order Markov, you will have different results when skipping duplicates. In order to check the effect of skipping duplicates in the first-order Markov chain, we will use my script for ``manual'' calculation because the package skips duplicates automatically.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\DocumentationTok{\#\#\#\#\# Higher order of Markov chains and consequent duplicated channels in the path \#\#\#\#\#}
 
\CommentTok{\# computing transition matrix {-} \textquotesingle{}manual\textquotesingle{} way}
\NormalTok{df\_multi\_paths\_m }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_multi\_paths }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
        \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{path =} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}(start) \textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, path, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textgreater{} (conversion)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{m }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{max}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{str\_count}\NormalTok{(df\_multi\_paths\_m}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textgreater{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)) }\SpecialCharTok{+} \DecValTok{1} \CommentTok{\# maximum path length}
 
\NormalTok{df\_multi\_paths\_cols }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ reshape2}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{colsplit}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{string =}\NormalTok{ df\_multi\_paths\_m}\SpecialCharTok{$}\NormalTok{path, }\AttributeTok{pattern =} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{} \textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{names =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\NormalTok{m))}
\FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(df\_multi\_paths\_cols) }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}ord\_\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\NormalTok{m))}
\NormalTok{df\_multi\_paths\_cols[df\_multi\_paths\_cols }\SpecialCharTok{==} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{] }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \ConstantTok{NA}
 
\NormalTok{df\_res }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{vector}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}list\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{ncol}\NormalTok{(df\_multi\_paths\_cols) }\SpecialCharTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
 
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ (i }\ControlFlowTok{in} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{:}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{ncol}\NormalTok{(df\_multi\_paths\_cols) }\SpecialCharTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{))) \{}
         
\NormalTok{        df\_cache }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_multi\_paths\_cols }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
                \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{num\_range}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ord\_"}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(i, i}\SpecialCharTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{))) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
                \FunctionTok{na.omit}\NormalTok{() }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
                \FunctionTok{group\_by\_}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{.dots =} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"ord\_"}\NormalTok{, }\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(i, i}\SpecialCharTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)))) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
                \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{n =} \FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{()) }\SpecialCharTok{\%\textgreater{}\%}
                \FunctionTok{ungroup}\NormalTok{()}
         
        \FunctionTok{colnames}\NormalTok{(df\_cache)[}\FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)] }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_from\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}channel\_to\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        df\_res[[i]] }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ df\_cache}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
